# Experiment ledger

One row per experiment. Columns:
- id
- paper / claim
- question
- procedure (sample, edit, control, metric)
- result (numbers)
- what it tells us
- effect on the thesis (supports / weakens / refines / neutral)

Earlier results (J1–J33, G1–G206) are in `distill-part/jld-features/research/FINDINGS.md` and are cited by id.

| id | paper/claim | question | status | result | tells us | thesis |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | A1, B | how many units per layer are dead, and how many change ≥1% of calls when set to their mean | done | 10,000 test jets (2,000/class), acc 81.29 %. Never firing / changing ≥1 % of calls: l1 49/64 · 13; P 50 · 11; g 49 · 7 (11 at ≥0.1 %); s 55 · 9; u/S 45 · 19; h4 9 · 42; h5 0 · 30; z 0 · 14. Largest single costs: a32 64.2 %, s14 64.9 %, u29 61.2 %, z1 21.6 % | the particle stage is about 13 cuts, the first objects about 11, the second-round objects 19; the post-pooling MLP is dense | supports A1 (small program) |
| E2 | A1 | each l1 unit as a cut in GeV and rad | done | live cuts (corrected by E16: 4 pT ramps; a4 and a50 are constant on data): pT ramps (a32: 3.86 − 0.352·pT, on for pT < 11.0 GeV, cost 64 %; a56: pT < 31.0 GeV; a15: 0.088·pT + 3.94, always on) and 9 angular half-planes through ≤0.01 rad of the axis in ~8 directions (a29: Δη > 0.007; a36: Δφ < −0.0024; a6: 0.62Δη + 0.78Δφ > 0.008 …), with zero pT coefficient | particle statements are "how soft" ramps and "which side of the axis" cuts; the angular objects are particle-count weighted, not pT weighted | supports A1; new: angular objects not IRC safe |
| E3 | A1, B | does Σ_i ReLU(w·x_i+b) = b·count + w·Σ_A x hold on the pooled layer P | done | exact (max error 0.0) on the network's own rounded inputs before the pooling quantizer; the pooling quantizer rounds each particle's value per slot (unit 32, slot 63: values snap to 2 or 4), mean |q(a)−a| 0.17–0.25. Variance split of P: P15 = ΣpT (share 1.00); P32 = 0.56 count + 0.44 ΣpT of particles below 11 GeV; angular P units = one-sided Σ Δη / Σ Δφ over particles on one side (shares 0.85–1.03) | each first-round object is one cut read by its count and summed (pT, Δη, Δφ); fixed-point rounding turns soft-particle ramps toward counts | supports A1/B (exact in float) |
| E3q | A1, B | does the decision depend on the fixed-point rounding of the first layers | done | same 10,000 jets; quantizers skipped: l1 input 2.62 % calls changed (acc 81.21 %); qs 1.48 % (81.43); qs1 2.76 % (80.88); l1+qs+qs1 3.83 % (81.02); all quantizers 51.6 % (43.71) | the particle and object stages can be written in float at a 3.8 % call cost; a later quantizer (likely the last layer's modulo wrap) is part of the computation | refines A1: "exact up to 3.8 % of calls" |
| E3w | A1, B | which quantizer does the all-float network (43.7 %) lack | done | 10,000 jets; float everywhere except one layer's quantizer: except l3 input 78.25 % (8.0 % calls changed), except qs1 78.83 % (8.1 %), every other single layer 39–54 %; float last layer d7 alone 0.30 % calls changed (its modulo wrap is not used) | the second-round quantizers carry something the network needs | led to E3m |
| E3m | A1, A3, B | are HGQ's zero-bit entries (fixed per slot × channel masks) the missing part | done | zero-bit fractions: qs 81.6 %, l2 79.4 %, dd 78.1 %, l3 80.1 %, qs1 84.2 %, d4 70.3 %, d5 12.5 %. Float + masks only: 80.68 % (fixed 81.29 %), 4.49 % calls changed; fully float 43.71 %. Slot windows of live S objects: u0, u43 = 14 hardest particles; u19 = slots 13–63; u1 = all but the leading particle; u29, u30, all 64 | the program is float arithmetic plus fixed membership masks: each object = cut ∧ pT-rank window; rounding and wrap are a 4.5 % perturbation | refines A1/A3: objects are defined by a cut AND a rank window |
| E8 | A6 | are classes conjunctions of neuron windows on JEDI's own z and S, and does imposing the conjunction cause the class | done; verified (E8v, confidence 0.6) | 30,000 jets (seed 4), trees on 20,000, evaluation 10,000. z rules (3 windows each): precision 95.8–98.4 %, coverage 69.5–82.8 %. Rule units set to in-rule medians on jets called otherwise: g 54.4, q 31.2, W 42.0, Z 62.0, t 25.9 %; all 16 neurons 100 %; random draws without a rule unit 1.4–4.1 % (all draws 10.2–12.4 %). Necessity (rule units → medians of other calls): g 0.0 %, q 95.3, W 22.9, Z 57.9, t 91.0 % still called c. At S: g 47.8, q 20.5, W 5.2, Z 14.7, t 33.1 % (W random 6.9 %). E8v (fresh disjoint 30,000 jets, seed 23): precision 96.2–98.4 %, coverage 69.7–82.0 %, switched 54.0/31.8/40.6/61.4/26.2 %; rule's set ranks 6/39/10/6/78 of all 560 z triples with the same value source (share of triples switching at least as much 0.011/0.070/0.018/0.011/0.14); highest triples 61.6/52.5/48.3/75.7/77.3 % (t: {z8, z10, z13}); triples without a rule unit mean 3.8–7.7 %, max 12.8–58.1 %; moving rule units just inside the windows 7.2–25.1 % (edge 3.4–12.8 %); 3-unit logit change = sum of single-unit changes (max diff 0.0); rules in 5 random rotations of z: precision 87–99 %, coverage 46–82 %; refit changes one unit for q, W, Z | the window rules describe the calls of unedited jets with ≥ 96 % precision, but similar rules exist in rotated bases; the causal effect follows how far the units move along d7's additive logit margin, not window membership; z0, z7 are E6's W and Z windows, z8 (124–170 GeV) is not E6's top window z10; E8's random control was not value-matched and its untrained control is degenerate (calls every jet t) | refines A6 (description holds; "imposing the conjunction causes the class" holds only for the typical-value edit) |
| E14 | A3, A4 | how round two builds near-radial objects from the nine half-planes, and whether the per-particle maps carry the direction term of the mass read-out | done; verified (E14v, confidence 0.7) | 10,000 jets (seed 8); float + masks program 80.30 %. 15 particle-level u units take half-plane input through five s units (s43, s60, s53, s32, s52); on-contours max/min 1.14–1.47; Part-1 weight polygon rms ≤ 0.005 for 60/78 contours (max 0.064). Translation δ 0.03: odd/even of the h5 mass read-out maps −0.143/−0.092/−0.173 vs measured (g frozen) −0.144/−0.096/−0.174 (η/φ/diag); point-symmetrized or θ-averaged maps ≤ 0.0008. One half-plane → 0: 7.0–29.0 % calls changed (g frozen), 99 % of the change on the own half. E14v (seed 31): Part 1/2 reproduce within 4 points; 11 single-path units, u13, u43, u40, u60 combine 2–3 s units; nonzero half-planes per unit 3–9 (all nine only u40); best circle rms 0.032–0.086, random directions 0.046–0.083 (below the circle in 6/17), real directions 0–0.026; odd(maps)/odd(measured, g frozen) 0.96–1.05, 95 % CIs within [0.93, 1.08]; sample-asymmetry term B −0.0005 to +0.0003 (p ≥ 0.20, H0 B = 0 not rejected); symmetrized network odd +0.0000 [−0.0005, +0.0005]; context share of odd part 15–24 % (seed 8: 9–21 %) | round two forms each near-radial object as the level set of a weighted sum of 3–9 half-planes in one to three s units; the contours are polygons only 3–9 % from a circle, so random-direction polygons fit about as well; the direction term of the mass read-out is the sum of the per-particle maps and vanishes when they are symmetrized; E14's hybrid counterfactual sends negative S into the unsigned fixed-point head (sign flip) and is replaced by the symmetrized-network construction | refines A3 (construction measured; not a nine-direction polygon); supports A4's odd-term explanation |
| E15 | A4, A9 | do h4/h5 combine M0 = ΣpT and M2 = Σ pT r² as a product or as a sum | done; refuted as stated by E15v (confidence 0.2) | 10,000 jets (seed 11). S0 responds to M0 only (e_k 0.00), S50 to M2 only (e_c 0.05). Separable factorial A2 (pT × c, angles × k/√c): read-out β (sum 0, product 1) 0.33/0.70/0.60/0.32 at S/h4/h5/z; M0-only ÷ pT×c response at h4 0.629/0.449/0.477 by M2 third (product 0.5 flat, sum 0.75/0.50/0.30). E15v (fresh 9,639 jets, seed 23): β 0.42/0.70/0.53/0.32; same read-out procedure on known populations: sum held linearly 0.00, ReLU windows on the sum −0.73 to −0.77, product 1.00, ReLU on separate M0, M2 0.90–1.15, random untrained ReLU layer on the trained S −0.01/0.42/1.14; read-outs of V_sum / V_prod from h4 give 0.03 / 0.71; refitting the read-out moves β by up to 0.09 (bootstrap se 0.0005–0.0008 omits this). Gain-normalised M0 response: S 0.62/0.58/0.55, h4 0.63/0.44/0.47, z 0.65/0.54/0.53; random ReLU layers on S 0.49–0.66/0.44–0.48/0.46–0.50. Matched M0×c vs M2×c steps (product d = 0; sum +0.47/0/−0.48): h4 median d +0.61/+0.15/+0.02, IQR width ≈ 1.5; the M0 step also moves pT-threshold features (calls differ between the two steps for 33.6 % of jets) | the β statistic does not separate sum from product; S holds M0 and M2 in separate units and the read-out matches the product within 0.06 for M2 > 8.1 GeV from S onward, which random ReLU layers on S also do; where the product forms (h4/h5) is not located by this test | weakens A4's "multiplied in h4/h5" and A9's product step (open); supports "read separately at S" |
| E10 | A9 | are high-level variables built over the layers, in order of algebraic depth | done (not yet skeptic-rerun; statistics in R2) | 20,000 jets (seed 0), 45 observables with a depth label, ridge + MLP read-outs at P, g, S, h4, h5, z; untrained control. Spearman depth vs first stage at R² ≥ 0.9: ρ 0.51 (p 4e-4); at 0.7: 0.38 (p 0.009). Depth 0–1 reach 0.9 at P or S; depth 2–3 mostly never and peak at h4. Causal: multiplicity edit, probe at S slope 0.52 (rotation control −0.004); girth edit slope 0.91 (control mean |Δ| 0.0018; side effect mass +18 GeV); D2 at fixed mass slope 0.057, corr 0.28 (control 0.30). Untrained R² ≤ 0.006 at S, h4. R2: ρ 0.507 [0.27, 0.71], permutation p 5e-4 | readability rises with depth; D2 is readable in data but its probe does not follow a D2 edit; readability is not use | supports A9 (readability); weakens "use" for depth ≥ 2 |
| E4 | A2 | how much of each second-round cut is context g, and does the decision need it | done; verified (E5v, confidence 0.9) | 20,000 jets (seed 0), acc 80.58 %; u = own + ctx + residual + bias (error 0.0). Context reaches 9 of 19 live u units (SD ratio u30 0.56, u17 0.29, rest ≤ 0.18). g → mean 14.90 % calls changed (acc 74.02 %); g → 0 51.4 %; another jet's g 28.0 %. g49 alone 10.38 %. Four objects (S0, S30, S31, S58) at mean-context values 14.44 %. g49 = 72 % P15 (ΣpT) | the context round carries ΣpT into S0 and three other objects; the decision needs it | supports A2 |
| E5 | A3 | are the second-round objects nameable (pT, r) hinges over rank windows, and do radial edits act as the hinge predicts | done; verified in corrected form (E5v, confidence 0.85 / 0.9) | 19 S units ≥ 0.5 % calls. Azimuth adds ≤ 0.017 R² (fit-based). S29: ReLU(4.44 ln pT + 93.8 r − 21.2) over all slots, 3-term name R² 0.976, cost 40.9 %; S1 = Σ_{ranks≥1}(r − 0.2)₊ R² 0.947; S0 = 0.32 pT + 1.40 over the 14 hardest (context 61 % of its variance); S50 ΣpT·r² over ranks 0–38 (0.938); S19 N(pT > 1) ranks 13–63 (0.991); S30 Σr for r < 0.165 (0.957). Radial crossing edits: S29 +0.482 vs predicted +0.469 (control +0.008); S1 +0.457 vs +0.446; random-r slope 1.03–1.04 | 17 of 19 objects are two-variable hinges with a fixed rank window; S31, S58 are functions of g only | supports A3 (v2) |

Rigid rotations / reflections change 3.73–4.08 % of calls (masked float 3.0–3.9 %). Ten particle objects move ≤ 0.1 SD at 180° (S0, S19, S20, S29, S30, S40, S43, S50, S51, S60); S13, S17, S63, S1 0.12–0.22 SD; S52, S56, S62 0.25–0.43 SD; S31, S58 0.41–0.44 SD. S29 edit slope 0.99 (hinge) vs 0.83 (3-term name). g → mean 14.61 %; four objects 13.91 %, same call as g → mean on 90.3 % of affected jets; 5 random sets of 4 from the other 15: 0.00–0.88 % | the S29 causal object is the hinge sum; the 3-term name is a correlational proxy; three particle objects are directional | refines A3 (v3); supports A2 |

| P3 | C2 (→ C2a, C2b), Paper C thesis v1 steps 2–3 (loop C round C1) | does class-attention head b2h8 switch near 85 GeV at full statistics with the corrected build, does it (with far-side heads) carry the mass into n98 (z[97]), and what does the literal circuit {b2h8, b1h3, n98} kept alone score | done; verified (P3v, P3v2; confidence 0.7) | ParT_full 85.93 % on the P1 sample (W/Z binary 83.25 %); ParT_kin 74.46 %. S = 2,000 jets/class (seed 31, random rows, _120 files), T = 10,000 W + 10,000 Z (_121). Labels: b2h8 = cls_blocks[1] head 7, n98 = z[97]. (a) b2h8 class-token share switch m50 87.10 GeV [86.79, 87.39], width 4.25, 0.86 → 0.08; span 82.5 pt (Holm p 0.016) vs next head 16.4; share change under φ + 0.06 (mass fixed) −0.171. (b) b2h8 mean ablation changes 0.85 % of W/Z calls (p 0.86), b1h3 12.1 %; G21b at full stats (angles × 1.1): Δz97 −0.345 = attention −0.118 (0.34) + value −0.236 + interaction 0.009. (c) literal circuits 52.9–54.1 % W/Z vs random circuits 49.7 % (p 0.01); n98 alone (intact class stage) 71.6 %; 10-class 10–13 %. Verifier rerun (P3v seed 97, _122/_123 files; P3v2 seed 113, _124): m50 86.64 [86.31, 87.01], span 80.6 vs 15.7; direct patch removal of the x1.1 Δz97: b2h8 0.046 [0.044, 0.047], b1h3 0.466 (0.43–0.53 over edits), all 8 block-1 heads 0.987, block 2 0.061; strict circuit with all 16 heads kept 63.0 % (unedited 82.85 %); particle heads 4 and 8 of every block remove 0.259 [0.252, 0.265] on W (stratified null 0.127 ± 0.035, p 0.016), Z 0.079 vs 0.045 (p 0.08), φ shift 0.061 vs 0.071 (p 0.61); all 64 particle heads 0.470 | corrected reading (P3v): b2h8 is the only class-attention head with a mass switch (87 GeV, 4 GeV wide; also moves under the mass-fixed φ shift), but patching it removes 3–5 % of n98's response to a real mass change; the eight block-1 class-attention heads remove 98–99 % (b1h3 43–53 %). P3's "b1h3 19 %" was the diluted resample slope. Particle far-side heads exceed random only on W jets under real mass edits, mostly head 4. The strict literal-circuit definition caps all 16 heads at 63 %, so the near-chance circuit score reflects the definition | weakens thesis v1 step 3; C2 split into C2a (switch exists) and C2b (route via block-1 heads) |

Rows 3000–4000 / 6000–7000 (10,000 each): PN_full 84.57 / 84.47 % (released 84.4 %), PN_kin 70.35 / 69.56 %, ParT_full 85.93 / 86.31 %, ParT_kin 74.46 / 73.99 %. φ + 0.06 (mass fixed): W → Z PN_full 12.3 → 28.5 % [25.8, 31.3] (McNemar 172/10), PN_kin 18.4 → 29.0 %, ParT_full 11.7 → 31.0 %, ParT_kin 16.2 → 37.3 %. Calibrated read-out ratio vs √(M0M2) prediction 0.62–0.71 (0 and 1 both rejected, Holm). Route shares of Δ(lW − lZ): ΔR alone 0.94–1.41, ΔR unedited |share| ≤ 0.06, Δη/Δφ ≤ 0.06, all kNN graphs fixed 0.96–1.01, layer-1 shortcut alone 0.85–1.05, centre 0.15–0.30, edge x_j − x_i ≤ 0.10. WTA inputs: 19.0 % (full) / 25.8 % (kin) calls changed. With particle attention, skip maps and embeddings computed on the real jet, ranked feature circuits reach 51 % W/Z at 50 features and 74–78 % at 1,000–2,000 (random 18–22 %); they are almost all class-level features; the live attention/skip paths alone give donor-level accuracy (17 %). With attention and skip maps also resampled, every variant stays at 42–43 %. The single m_A-correlated feature does not carry the φ response at calibrated steps or under ablation; at 4–8 s.d. it moves calls toward W. Joint steering reproduces the natural m_A read-out slope, which is about 1/7 of the φ edit's logit change. P13's single-feature steering criterion (≥ 0.6 of φ) could not be met under its own calibration. Conclusion: learned sparse features ranked by attribution did not give a small circuit for ParT_full's W-versus-Z decision; 50 features kept alone give about 51 % against the model's 83 %, and 75 % needs 1,000–2,000 features, only while attention and the transcoders' linear bypass are computed on the real jet. The axis-moment-mass information is not in one feature; moving all features of a class-token layer together reproduces how the score varies with m_A in natural jets, which is about a seventh of the φ-shift response; trained (not ranked) feature circuits are untested | weakens the P13 thesis (C8 reverses with learned features); supports C8 at the feature level (ranked selection); C7: single-feature coupling not supported |

The EdgeConv-1 direction fitted on + shifts records the sign of the shift and reproduces −0.2 to −0.3 of the − response; the EdgeConv-2 direction is sign-symmetric and reproduces 0.73–0.80 of each sign; one-direction matches of this size also appear in shuffled or freshly initialised networks, so they do not show a trained variable. Pooled 32 (full) / 16 (kin) channels kept alone match W/Z accuracy on three samples but not 10-class accuracy (selection by W-vs-Z attribution). No basis-alignment statement holds (sign depends on the rotation control). EFP / Lund probes cannot respond to a φ shift; gradient and LRP give per-jet r 0.31–0.45 (DAS r not comparable). Conclusion: ParticleNet's W-versus-Z score responds the same way to a mass-preserving φ shift of either sign; a direction after the second graph layer reproduces about three quarters of each, but directions like it also exist in untrained networks, and it is not a copy of the axis-moment mass. The rebuilt neighbour graphs carry at most about 4 % of the response. A few dozen pooled channels kept alone reproduce the W/Z calls but not the 10-class calls | refines C5 (graphs ≤ 4 %); new C9; C7 analogue not supported as a trained m_A variable; adds a weight-shuffled control (ParT's P8 had an untrained control, 0.02–0.31 vs 0.58–0.69 trained; a weight-shuffled ParT control was not run) |

Added control, 8 untrained inits: W → Z −0.3 to +4.0 pt (kin init0 +4.0, McNemar p 2e-42); block-1 share 0.32–0.87; R −0.23 to 0.78; no strong S4 switch. Strong class-share switches in 4 of 6 seeds (full s1 c2h2 105 GeV, full s2 c2h4 68 GeV, kin s1 c2h1 64 / c2h3 85 GeV, kin s2 c2h3 85 GeV, 0.85 → 0.11, amplitude 0.74 vs b2h8 0.79) | corrected reading (P11v): in all six retrained seeds, on two disjoint samples, φ + 0.06 moves true W to Z by +13.6 to +19.8 points; untrained networks −0.3 to +4.0. R lies between 0 and 1 in every seed, but untrained networks also give R −0.23 to 0.78, so R's value is not a training signature. S1 holds in 3/6 seeds and does not separate trained from untrained; S2 is structural. S3 fails in 6/6 (0.63–0.84); the seeds lie inside the untrained range (0.32–0.87); only the released 0.97–0.98 exceeds every untrained value. The released single block-1 head share 0.46 is not reproduced (seeds ≤ 0.23). Strong switch heads in 4/6 seeds, at different heads and masses; kin s2 c2h3 (85 GeV) is as large as b2h8. Seed, distinct-data scale (2M vs 100M jets) and total training (51M vs 512M samples) remain confounded. Conclusion: six Particle Transformers retrained from scratch all keep the released network's main behaviour on two test samples: rotating every particle about the jet axis, which leaves the jet mass unchanged, turns 14–20 % more true W jets into Z, while untrained networks barely respond. Inside, the retrained models split the mass response between the two class-attention layers at levels untrained networks already reach, whereas the released checkpoints send almost all of it through the first; sharp mass-switching heads appear in some retrained models at different heads and masses. Seed and training scale are not separated | supports C3s at the call level (0.3 → 0.7, reworded); refines C2a / C2b to the released checkpoints; S1 not a training test |

Arm c gives R 0. Planted arms take 79–83 % of a real mass change through the pair features, the released models 82–84 % through the per-particle angles. ParT with these inputs can learn a mostly rotation-invariant mass. Labels a and b coincide on 97.7 % of jets and the arms share initialisation and data order, so arm b does not test whether the label sets the mass read; "H_data strict excluded" is withdrawn. Source of the released models' larger R (10 classes, data size, training length, seed) not identified; planted R moves 0.12–0.24 across epochs. Arm s collapsed (its bars in p14_planted_p16.png are noise). Conclusion: Particle Transformers trained from scratch to flag a jet-mass window respond to a rotation that leaves the invariant mass unchanged at about 0.2 of their response to an equal real mass change, against about 0.75 for the released models, on fresh jets and with two read-outs. So the architecture with these inputs can learn a mostly rotation-invariant mass and does not force the axis-relative response. The axis-moment-mass label equals the invariant-mass label on almost every real jet, so whether the training target sets which mass is read is not shown, and which part of the released training produces the larger response is not identified | refines C10 (v1 pre-registration: R_a < 0.2 holds, R_b ≈ 1 not observed; H_arch excluded; reworded v2, 0.55); C3 reproduced on fresh rows; method validation: 1-d DAS and EAP did not separate arms whose labels coincide on 97.7 % |

Untrained-net DAS (seed 1018) emb / b4 / b8: scramble 0.33 / 0.53 / 0.52, soft5 0.51 / 0.63 / 0.62, phi 0.61 / 0.64 / 0.62; untrained IIA 0.45–0.95, above ParT_full in 44 of 55 cells. Difference-in-differences vs untrained (full): phi −0.20, dil −0.15, pT −0.18, per-particle scramble +0.18 [0.14, 0.22], soft5 +0.007 [−0.025, 0.040] (p 0.66; kin +0.11). Untrained mass R² margin 0.04–0.07 (seed 18), 0.085–0.13 (seed 1018). **Rerun 2** (_102, seed 2018, N 5,000): full-jet scramble (pair features also scrambled; 46.2 % calls changed vs 5.9 % per-particle only) IIA emb / b4 / b7 0.02 / 0.59 / 0.68; untrained flat 0.45 / 0.53 from b1; DiD +0.45 [0.43, 0.47] and +0.44 [0.42, 0.47] (full), +0.43 / +0.42 (kin) | corrected reading (P18v): N and log pT readable at the embedding (R² 0.99), the masses at b1 (0.94), no later increase; untrained ParT reaches 0.99 for N, pT and 0.80–0.89 for the masses (margin 0.04–0.13). Readable order (N, pT) emb → mass, tau21 b1 → tau32 b2–b4; H1's mass-last order not observed (fresh-row mass − prong t90 −0.38 / −0.42 sites). Only tau21 / tau32 read-outs depend on training (scramble gain 0.77–0.80 vs 0.00–0.05 untrained). 1-d DAS levels are not training evidence (untrained 0.45–0.95); training-specific are the depth profiles: fall with depth for pT, dilation, φ (DiD −0.15 to −0.20) and rise for the azimuth scramble (per-particle 0.01 → 0.38, DiD +0.15 to +0.18; full-jet 0.02 → 0.68 at b6–b7 vs flat 0.45–0.54 untrained, DiD +0.44). Soft-particle rise training-specific only in ParT_kin. The permuted-net control (rows permuted without the next layer's inputs, accuracy 10–12 %) is a weak null. Conclusion: in ParT, jet multiplicity and pT can be read from the input embedding and the jet mass from the first block on, but an untrained network of the same shape gives nearly the same read-outs, so these read-outs do not show learning and there is no mass-last stage. Prong-shape variables (tau21, tau32) are read only by the trained network, and the share of the response to randomising each particle's azimuth about the axis that one direction carries rises from about 0.02 at the embedding to about 0.7 by blocks 6–7, against a flat 0.5 in untrained networks. The soft-radial edit (D2 −4 %) moves ParT_full +0.31 vs the unedited jet (the reported +0.73 is vs a rotation that itself moves −0.42); through the mass-restoring dilation it raises the hard-particle mass by about 16 %, and a reference with that mass predicts +0.29 on average (not class by class). Block / head patching in blocks 5–8 gives the same profile and leading heads for prong edits and observable-neutral rotations, so it does not locate an observable-specific stage; DAS shows a training-specific late rise for E1 and E3 above their rotations and well below the full scramble. Conclusion: ParT does not respond to τ21, τ32 or D2 at fixed jet mass the way classifiers built on these variables do: narrowing the two prongs of W, Z and top jets makes ParT_full less W-like, where a (mass, τ21) classifier predicts the opposite, while QCD jets follow the classifier. The large response to moving soft particles inward is about +0.3 against the unedited jet and may come from the hard-particle mass the edit also raises. W → Z: t 19.0 → 24.9 %, u 18.4 → 18.7 % (p_Holm 0.10), released kin 17.9 → 42.0 %, full 11.2 → 32.5 %. Per-particle share t 0.296, u 0.096, released 0.826 / 0.842. Over epochs 0 → 9: R(φ+) t 0.139 → 0.221, t2 0.067 → 0.251; R(φ−) t 0.734 → 0.470, t2 0.933 → 0.491 | corrected reading (P22v): the planted arms' R depends on the shift direction (0.18–0.26 for φ+, 0.41–0.49 for φ−, 0.21–0.26 for η+); the released models give 0.73–0.86 in all three. The pre-registered thesis range (R_t 0.6–0.8) is excluded in every direction; alt 1 (R_t ≈ R_a) holds for φ+ (t − a −0.01 to +0.06 over directions); alt 2 (R_u ≈ R_t) excluded (R_u ≤ 0.02 at equal accuracy, 99 % of the dilation response kept). R does not approach the released level over 10 epochs. Route share and R are in the same order only between planted and released models and for u, not within the planted arms (a2 R 0.263 > t 0.221, share 0.22 < 0.30). R_probe in the JSON is uncalibrated and unused. Bootstrap CIs cover jets only; seed spread 0.02–0.08. Conclusion: Particle Transformers trained on 1.5 million W, Z and QCD jets with the true labels respond to a mass-preserving shift of the jet axis only 0.2–0.5 times as strongly as to a matched real mass change, depending on the shift direction, against 0.73–0.86 for the released JetClass models in every direction, and they do not approach that level over training. Training with random axis offsets removes the response (ratio about 0.01) at the same accuracy, so the architecture does not force it. Which difference in the released training (100M jets, 10 classes or many more optimisation steps) produces the larger response is untested | weakens the node thesis (true labels at 1.5M do not reproduce the released R); refines C10 (v3: true labels and window labels both give R ≪ released; offset augmentation removes it); C3s: released response needs training beyond 3 classes at 1.5M |
| P23 | C3, C7, C5/C9 → C12 (new), D4, A8 (loop C round C5, exploratory) | is ParT's response to a rigid axis offset an even axis-moment (M2) term or a direction-dependent (odd, centroid-linear) term as in JEDI-linear; ParT vs ParticleNet | done; verified with corrections (P23v, confidence 0.62; jobs c-p23-verify, c-p23-verify2) | jobs anrunw-ai-c-p23-run (GPU 3,338 s, first attempt evicted), c-p23b-perjet (CPU), c-p23-smoke, c-p23-plot; scripts interp/p23_axis_surface.py, interp/p23b_perjet.py. Sample: test_20M W/Z/ZJetsToNuNu _105, 3,000 random rows each (default_rng(2305)), N 9,000. Models ParT_full/kin, P10 seeds s1/s2, PN_full/kin, two untrained ParT. Edits: rigid translation on a 9 × 9 grid \|c_i\| ≤ 0.08 rad (40 mirror pairs), 4 random directions at \|c\| 0.06, rotations 90/180/270°, matched dilation k_j. Read-out d = Δ(logit_Z − logit_W); odd/even from mirror pairs; gains g = b/κ (κ = response per unit ½dlog M2 under dilation). P1-row accuracy 85.93 / 74.83 % (kin; P4 74.46 %, unexplained), PN 84.57 / 70.34 %, seeds 83.6 / 83.5 %, untrained 12.7 / 11.9 %. g_odd vs g_even: ParT_full 0.68 [0.56, 0.79] vs 0.66 [0.64, 0.67] (p 0.71); ParT_kin 0.02 vs 0.50; s1 0.33 vs 0.59; s2 0.63 vs 0.74; PN_full 0.52 vs 0.27; PN_kin 0.71 vs 0.15. Odd variance share: ParT 0.064–0.110, PN 0.16–0.18, untrained 0.57–0.67. φ + 0.06 mean (ParT_full): total 0.760, even 0.744. Even R²: pooled 0.31, per-jet κ_j 0.72, per-jet quadratic 0.87. Per-jet odd gradient vs own pT centroid cos 0.132 vs null 0.125 (p 0.07). Long-vs-short even: ParT_full −0.098, PN −0.63 / −1.53. R to matched dilation ParT_full 0.78–0.82, PN_full 0.47–0.58. W → Z (ParT_full) φ + 0.06 10.4 → 31.0 %, rot90 13.2 %. **P23v rerun** (_107, seed 2399, W/Z N 4,000): ParT_full odd share 0.063 [0.059, 0.066], g_o 0.68, g_e 0.65, φ + 0.06 total 0.755 / even 0.749, W → Z 9.9 → 29.3 %, R 0.79–0.84; κ_j control even R² own 0.72, other-jet 0.10, rotation scale 0.11, none 0.32; Σ pT\|r+c\|^b per-jet R² b = 2 0.88, b = 1 0.82, b = 3 0.85 (untrained b = 2 0.92). **P23v2** (_108, seed 2311, N 4,000): corr(Po, tanh\|η_jet\|) −0.944, M1η < 0 in 98.3 % of jets; g_odd raw → axis-only inputs ParT_full 0.69 → 0.59, PN_full 0.66 → 0.57 (Holm 0.005), odd share change ≤ 0.009; per-jet φ-axis odd share ParT_full 0.075 [0.071, 0.081], ParT_kin 0.100, PN_full 0.180, PN_kin 0.169, untrained 0.650; odd-gradient cos: ParT_kin vs leading particle 0.130 (null −0.021, p 0.001), ParT_full vs unweighted centroid 0.053 (0.007, p 0.004), PN_full unweighted 0.185, leading −0.201; within-jet s.d. across directions at \|c\| 0.06 0.33 vs mean 0.75 (ParT_full); per-jet even-anisotropy median 0.15 (ParT_full), 0.47 (PN_full) | corrected reading (P23v): about 92 % of each jet's response variance to a rigid φ shift is symmetric under c → −c (92–94 % over the grid); P23's "98 % of the mean" is near-guaranteed by φ-reflection symmetry and is not used. Among Σ pT\|r+c\|^b, b = 2 (M2) fits best for trained nets, and the per-jet size follows the jet's own dilation response (R² 0.72 vs 0.10); a quadratic symmetric shape alone is generic (untrained fits it too). The antisymmetric part (6–10 %) does not follow the pT-weighted centroid but weakly the leading particle (ParT_kin) or unweighted centroid (ParT_full). P23's pooled g_odd is a gain on a regressor 94 % determined by jet \|η\| (13–27 % from η-shift energy changes), not a centroid gain. ParticleNet antisymmetric share 16–19 %, aligned with the unweighted centroid and away from the leading particle, and its symmetric part follows the dilation less (R² 0.37 / 0.18). At fixed \|c\| the response varies with direction (s.d. 0.33 vs mean 0.75), unexplained by M2 or the jet's long axis; "10 %" understated. Conclusion: when all of a jet's particles are shifted by a small angle with the mass unchanged, about 93 % of ParT's per-jet response is the same for a shift and its mirror image, and its size follows the jet's response to a real widening of its angles, as expected if ParT reads the angular second moment about the stored axis. ParticleNet has a larger mirror-asymmetric part (about 18 %), and in neither network does that part follow the pT-weighted centroid that JEDI-linear uses. A substantial dependence on the shift's direction at fixed size remains unexplained | refines C3 (axis response is an even, M2-like term per jet); new C12 (0.62); bears on C7's 1/7 gap (the φ shift acts through the even term; gap not explained by an odd term); C5 / A8: response shape differs between ParT and ParticleNet |
| P26 | C3s / P20 (data scale) → C13 (new), C3 (loop C round C6, exploratory) | does Sophon (ParT architecture, released weights, trained on JetClass-II with 188 classes, mass-decorrelated) keep the axis-relative mass response | done; verified (P26v, confidence 0.75; job c-p26-verify) | jobs anrunw-ai-c-p26-run2 (final), c-p26-run, c-p26-smoke, c-p26-plot; scripts interp/p26_sophon_axis.py, interp/sophon_part.py (copied from weaver-core), interp/p26_fetch.sh, interp/p26_plot.py; weights /j-jepa-vol/aaron-interp/models/sophon (jet-universe/sophon model.pt, 2.305 M parameters, 8 + 2 blocks; ONNX check max \|Δsoftmax\| 6e-6, top-1 agreement 100 % on 200 jets). Inputs per JetClassII_full.yaml (17 features, four-vectors × 500/jet_pT). Read-out d = log p(Z group: X→bb, cc, ss) − log p(W group: X→bc, cs, bq, cq, sq) (X→qq excluded); ridge probe of log m_inv on the 128-d class token. Sample A: JetClass test_20M W/Z/ZJetsToNuNu _112, 5,000 random rows per class (seed 2601). Sample B: JetClass-II Res2P_0250–0253 + QCD_0350, pT 500–1000 GeV, 70 < m_X < 100 GeV, N 5,496 (seed 2602). Edits: φ + 0.06, matched dilation, whole-jet rotation null, φ with ΔR or Δφ unedited (routes), WTA re-axis with same-length random axis move and ΔR-keeping rotation as controls. References ParT_full/kin, two untrained Sophon inits; permutation tests, Holm over 21. P1 accuracy ParT 0.8593 / 0.7447. Sophon mapped 3-class A 0.626 [0.618, 0.634], B 0.752; resonance vs QCD 0.831 / 0.867; 188-class top-1 on B 0.4995; ParT_full mapped A 0.824, B 0.710. Dilation Δd on W jets / Z−W separation: Sophon 0.016 (A), ≈ 0 (B); ParT_full 0.278. R output: Sophon −0.256 [−0.308, −0.207] (A), −0.006 (B); ParT_full 0.801 / 0.759; ParT_kin 0.734 / 0.626; rotation null ≤ 0.016. R_probe: Sophon 0.125 [0.109, 0.140] (A), 0.252 (B); ParT_full 0.776 / 0.971; ParT_kin 1.507 / 0.786; untrained 0.844 / 0.563. W → Z under φ: Sophon 24.3 → 23.5 % (A, Holm p 0.012); ParT_full 11.5 → 32.9 %; ParT_kin 17.9 → 41.7 %. WTA top-1 changed (WTA / random axis move / rotation): Sophon A 7.3 / 6.6 / 16.0 %, B 6.8 / 6.1 / 18.3 %; ParT_full 21.8 / 28.2 / 8.4 %; ParT_kin 34.4 / 40.7 / 9.5 %. **P26v rerun** (A' _113 seed 2699 N 15,000; B' Res2P_0254–0258 + QCD_0351 seed 2698 N 6,000): Sophon acc3 0.622 / 0.737; dil/sep 0.018 / −0.001; R −0.322 / −0.026; R_probe 0.179 [0.163, 0.197] / 0.232; probe calibration 0.46 / 0.56 (untrained 0.15, R² 0.26); probe-free token projection R_h −0.298 [−0.310, −0.286] / −0.121 (ParT_full 0.843 / 0.89); route split of the dilation probe response four-vector / angular 0.40 / 0.57 (A'), 0.47 / 0.50 (B'), ParT_full 0.05 / 0.95; Sophon W → Z 25.4 → 23.8 % (p 1.5e-7); WTA / random move / rotation 7.2 / 6.7 / 16.6 % (A', p 0.03), 6.9 / 6.7 / 17.7 % (B', p 0.50); paired bootstrap R_probe Sophon − ParT_full −0.62 [−0.64, −0.60] (A'), −0.75 (B'), R_h −1.14 / −1.02, all Holm 0.008 | corrected reading (P26v): Sophon's flavour-group W/Z output barely changes under a real 12 % mass increase (1.6–1.8 % of the Z−W separation on JetClass, ≈ 0 on JetClass-II), so output R is a ratio of small numbers. Its class-token mass probe moves under φ + 0.06 at 0.13–0.25 of its dilation movement (ParT_full 0.78–0.98, untrained 0.83 with poor calibration); the probe-free token projection gives −0.30 / −0.12. 50–57 % of the probe's dilation response enters through the per-particle angular inputs (ParT_full 95 %), so Sophon's size reading is largely insensitive to the axis offset but is not a pure four-vector invariant-mass reading ("mostly invariant mass representation" withdrawn). WTA re-axing changes Sophon's calls at the level of a same-length random axis move; ΔR-keeping rotations change more, so that rotation is not an informative control for Sophon. Its output moves under the φ shift opposite to the dilation (2.6× the dilation size). Scale, mass decorrelation and pT-normalised four-vectors are confounded. Conclusion: Sophon, a Particle Transformer released after training on the much larger JetClass-II dataset, hardly changes its W/Z output when the jet mass is really increased, so the output-level axis test has nothing to measure. Inside, its mass-like read-out moves about 0.2 as much under a mass-preserving axis shift as under a real mass change, against 0.8–1.0 for the JetClass ParT; about half of its size reading still uses the axis-relative angles, so it reads jet size in a way that mostly ignores where the axis sits rather than reading the invariant mass. Whether data scale, mass decorrelation or pT-normalised inputs causes the difference is open | refines C3s/C3: the axis-relative mass reading is not a property of the ParT architecture; new C13 (0.7); node thesis (R 0.5–1.0, WTA ≥ 10 %) excluded; the alternative's "dilation response kept" not met |
| P27 | A8, C12, P12 → C14 (new), C5 (loop C round C6, exploratory) | per-particle kernel of the axis response in ParT and ParticleNet: does each particle's response to a small radial move follow ∂M2 = 2 pT r, or JEDI-style pT-rank windows / thresholds | done; verified with corrections (P27v, confidence 0.6; jobs c-p27-verify, c-p27-verify-ana2) | jobs anrunw-ai-c-p27-run2 (GPU 400 s), c-p27-ana (CPU 76 s); script interp/p27_particle_kernel.py, verify interp/p27v_particle_kernel_verify.py. Models ParT_full/kin, PN_full/kin; controls untrained ParT_full (seed 2701), ParT_kin with rows of every weight tensor permuted. Sample: test_20M WToQQ_106 + ZToQQ_106, 2,000 random rows each (rng 2701), N 4,000 jets, 97,370 particles with pT share > 0.5 %. Edits (dr 0.01 rad, pT kept, all inputs incl. pair features rebuilt): each particle outward / inward; control (a) same particle rotated about the axis at fixed r; 6 random pairs per jet; control (b) total displacement spread as dr/5 over 5 random particles; all particles outward (calibration); unedited re-run (floor ≤ 1.1e-6). d = Δ(ℓ_Z − ℓ_W). Forms (global coefficients): M2 (x = dM2/M2), M2 with score-dependent gain, M2 per rank window, per-window (z, r, zr), pT-share hinges, radius hinges, pT-only, r-only, boosted tree; targets outward, odd (out − in)/2, rad − az; 5-fold held-out R², binned R², BIC; 1,000 jet bootstraps; Holm over 108. P1 10-class accuracy ParT 85.93 / 74.83 %, PN 84.57 / 70.34 %; W+Z sample 75.1 / 62.5 / 73.0 / 59.2 %. Values ParT_full / ParT_kin / PN_full / PN_kin: mean d_rad 0.027 [0.025, 0.029] / 0.024 / 0.022 / 0.020; even share of per-particle variance 0.47 / 0.45 / 0.52 / 0.24; \|az\|/\|rad\| 0.70 / 0.73 / 0.81 / 0.71; signed azimuthal mean 0.000 (p 0.44–0.99). Held-out R² M2 odd part 0.030 [0.024, 0.036] / 0.040 / 0.031 / 0.068, outward 0.019 / 0.035 / 0.018 / 0.078; largest rank-window / threshold gain over M2 +0.012 (PN_kin, Holm p 5e-12); tree 0.064 / 0.110 / 0.067 / 0.223; binned R² M2 0.93 / 0.91 / 0.95 / 0.91 (untrained −3.2). Pair additivity 0.947 [0.900, 0.978] / 0.968 / 0.939 / 0.956; sum of singles / whole-jet 1.003 [0.951, 1.063] / 1.062 / 0.888 / 1.017. Untrained mean \|d\| 0.0014 (r-only kernel); permuted ParT_kin R² ≈ 0 for every form (calls 99.8 % W). **P27v rerun** (WToQQ_107 + ZToQQ_107, 1,000 random rows each, rng 2777, 48,628 particles): mean d_rad 0.029 [0.027, 0.032] / 0.024 / 0.025 / 0.021; even share 0.46 / 0.44 / 0.49 / 0.24; held-out R² M2 outward 0.028 / 0.039 / 0.022 / 0.082; pt_only − M2 −0.001 to −0.006; pt_only binned R² 0.96–1.00 (M2 0.91–0.95). Within-z-decile slope on dM2/M2 relative to the M2-only slope: 0.65 / 0.60 / 0.78 / 0.63 (outward), 0.72 / 0.69 / 0.74 / 0.74 (odd); ratio 0 rejected p < 1e-17, ratio 1 rejected p_Holm 4.6e-3 to 1e-22. Route split (additivity R² 0.985–0.995): ParT pair-input-only route carries 0.96 / 0.98 of the even mean square (\|az\|/\|rad\| 0.93–0.94); per-particle-only route 0.75 / 0.80 of the mean response, even share 0.01–0.04; ParticleNet 97–99 % through per-particle (EdgeConv) features, not kNN coordinates. Even/odd variance ratio at 0.003 vs 0.01 rad: 0.38 / 1.72 / 0.43 / 0.69 (smooth prediction 0.09, p 2e-5 to 5e-69); ParT per-particle route 0.12–0.14 | corrected reading (P27v): the mean single-particle response rises with pT share and, at fixed pT share, with r; the within-z slope on dM2/M2 is 0.6–0.8 of the M2-only slope (both 0 and 1 excluded), so "in proportion to M2" is too strong. Per particle, M2, pT-only, r-only, rank-window and threshold forms all reach held-out R² ≤ 0.08; about half of the per-particle variance is even under out vs in and tracks the fixed-r rotation response; in ParT this component is carried by the pair inputs (96–98 %) and is not smooth (does not shrink as step²). In ParticleNet it goes through the per-particle EdgeConv features. No signed azimuthal term in any model. The rank-window vs M2 comparison (ΔR² ≤ 0.012) is made where every form explains < 10 %, so it is weak evidence on A8; rank windows were not fitted on the smooth ParT per-particle route (M2 R² 0.09–0.14 there). Pair additivity, calibration and the untrained / permuted controls were not rerun. Conclusion: moving one particle slightly away from the jet axis shifts the W-versus-Z score of ParT and ParticleNet, and on average the shift grows with the particle's momentum share and its distance from the axis, at about 0.6–0.8 of the steepness that exact proportionality to the axis moment M2 would give. Particle by particle no simple formula predicts the shift, and JEDI-style momentum-rank windows do no better than M2. In ParT most of the scatter comes from the pair features between particles and responds in a non-smooth way; what that pair term reads, and whether rank windows appear in the smooth per-particle part, is untested | new C14 (0.55); refines C12 (M2 reading holds for sums and means, not per particle); weakens A8 at the per-particle level (weak evidence); C5: no per-particle signed azimuthal term in ParticleNet |

W → Z under φ+: t 19.9 → 26.2 % (+6.3), t2 +7.3, m10s 21.0 → 30.5 % (+9.5 [8.5, 10.5], McNemar p_Holm 1e-92), released kin +23.6, full +22.1. Per-particle route share of the dilation t 0.294, t2 0.308, m10s 0.377, released 0.826 / 0.839. Interim (lr not decayed) R φ+ / φ− / η+: L@0 0.06 / 0.76 / −0.10, L@4 0.26 / 0.43 / 0.15, L@9 0.33 / 0.46 / 0.21, L@12 0.28 / 0.52 / 0.36; m10@2 0.35 / 0.43 / 0.44; m10s@0 0.09 / 0.70 / −0.12 | At fixed 1.5M jets and steps, the 10-class objective raises R by about 0.1 (φ+, η+), lowers φ− by 0.04, raises the per-particle route share 0.29–0.31 → 0.38 and the φ+ W → Z change 6–7 → 9.5 points, at unchanged 3-class accuracy; all remain below the released values (R 0.72–0.87, share 0.83, +22–24 points). One seed per arm; the permutation test resamples jets with networks fixed, so the seed comparison rests on the t/t2 pair. Conclusion: a Particle Transformer trained on all 10 JetClass classes (1.5M jets, the same step count as the 3-class runs) responds to a rigid shift of the jet about its axis about 0.3 times as strongly as to a real mass change of the same size; the 3-class runs give about 0.22 and the released models 0.72–0.87. The 10-class objective raises the axis response measurably but at this data size accounts for only about 0.1 of the 0.5–0.65 gap. The 5M-jet 10-class run and the 5×-longer 3-class run are still training | refines C10 (class-count thesis excluded for m10s: R < 0.5 and asymmetric in φ±; the 10-class objective is one contributing factor); alternatives 1 (steps) and 2 (data volume) open until m10 and L finish; next-round command pre-registered in inbox_c/merged/P31_report.md. Round C9 final (P39 d, same _114 rows, N 15,000): R φ+ / φ− / η+ m10 (10 classes, 5.0M jets, 10 epochs) 0.350 [0.340, 0.361] / 0.411 / 0.420, L (3 classes, 50 epochs) 0.293 [0.276, 0.310] / 0.309 / 0.351; per-particle route share m10 0.36, L 0.27 (released 0.83); W → Z under φ+ m10 20.5 → 31.3 %, L 23.2 → 31.3 %; m10 − t +0.135 / −0.061 / +0.202, L − t +0.078 / −0.163 / +0.133, all p_Holm 0.0075 (15 tests). Pre-registered outcome: class count (R(m10) ≥ 0.5) not met, steps (R(L) toward 0.7) not met, data volume m10 > m10s by +0.035 (φ+) and +0.117 (η+); Alt 3 (no arm reaches 0.5) holds. Conclusion (final): neither 3.3× more data with 10 classes nor 5× more training steps brings our ParT_kin trainings above about 0.42 of a real mass change, against 0.72–0.87 for the released models, so what produces the released models' larger axis response is not identified within our training code path → C10 v5 (0.65) |

Models ParT_full/kinpid/kin, PN_full/kinpid. Sample test_20M _115, default_rng(3215): 5,000 HToWW2Q1L + 556 random rows per other class, N 10,004. Lepton candidate = highest-pT particle with isElectron/isMuon (4,926 jets with candidate and matched hadron). Edits e1 flag → charged hadron; e2 (control) matched hadron gets the lepton flag; e3 lepton merged into nearest charged hadron (e3c control); e4 lepton pT ×0.5 / ×2 at fixed ΣpT; e5 muon/electron flag on the leading hadron of 1,668 QCD/H4q/Tbqq jets. Accuracy P1 sample: ParT_full 0.8593, ParT_kin 0.7447, ParT_kinpid 0.7902, PN_full 0.8456, PN_kinpid 0.7720; 10-class on this sample 0.916 [0.910, 0.921] / 0.799 / 0.867 / 0.906 / 0.847. e1 calls changed: ParT_full 0.987 (e2 0.145; McNemar 4,160 vs 9 jets; Δlog p −7.23 [−7.29, −7.17] vs −0.40), ParT_kinpid 0.985 (0.228), PN_full 0.986 (0.116), PN_kinpid 0.983 (0.247), ParT_kin 0 exactly. e3 0.95–0.98 (ParT_kin 0.879) vs e3c 0.013–0.050; e4 halving 0.035–0.099 (ParT_kin 0.278). e5mu ParT_full 0.092 [0.080, 0.108] vs 0.012. ParT_full b2h1 attention on lepton 0.993; single-head lepton-key knock-out b2h1 −5.38 [−5.47, −5.29] log-odds; 37 of 80 heads Holm-significant; joint knock-out all 80 heads 94.7 % calls changed (control 1.3 %), 16 class heads 11.0 %, set S (37) 78.4 % (random 37-head sets −5.44, p 0.0099). Top-3 circuit (b2h1, p7h8, p8h6; MLPs and embeddings kept, other heads mean-ablated) H→ℓνqq′ recall 0.973 [0.969, 0.977], 10-class 0.550; class-9 circuit 0.989 / 0.855. ParT_kinpid b2h4 attention 0.993, knock-out −2.39; ParT_kin b2h4 0.955, −1.80. PN: lepton kNN neighbours changed under e1 at EdgeConv 2/3 0.023/0.043; graph held fixed keeps 99.6 % of e1. **P32v re-analysis** (results/part/p32_verify_reanalysis.json): top-3 circuit calls 551/556 t→bℓν jets and 128/4,448 other jets H→ℓνqq′; balanced accuracy 0.212 vs random 3-head sets mean 0.145, 95th pct 0.222 (p 0.12); class-9 circuit 0.748 vs random 0.820 (p 0.92); 37-head 0.470 vs 0.703 (p 1.0); ParT_kinpid top-3 balanced 0.179 (p 0.14), 536/556 t→bℓν called H→ℓνqq′; ParT_kinpid b2h7 attention 0.974 with knock-out 0.00; e2 changes go 626/714 to t→bℓν (two-lepton jet); class-head-only 11 %, particle-head-only 6 %, both 95 % (redundant routes); jets without a lepton candidate (N 73) still called H→ℓνqq′ 53 % | corrected reading (P32v): in the four PID models removing the e/μ flag from the leading lepton candidate changes 98–99 % of H→ℓνqq′ calls; giving a matched hadron a lepton flag (e2, not a null: it creates a two-lepton jet) changes 12–25 %, mostly to t→bℓν. The flag is necessary, not sufficient (flag on the leading hadron of QCD/H4q/Tbqq moves 9 %). In each ParT model one class head (b2h1 full; b2h4 kinpid, kin) attends 0.96–0.99 to the lepton and gives the largest single-head knock-out; attention alone does not pick it out (kinpid b2h7 attends 0.97, no effect). The lepton reaches the class token through two redundant routes (class heads alone 11 %, particle heads alone 6 %, both 95 %). Head circuits (MLPs kept) keep recall but call 99 % of t→bℓν jets H→ℓνqq′ and have balanced accuracy no higher than random head sets; they are not literal circuits under the user definition (MLPs, embeddings kept). ParT_kin responds to the lepton's kinematics; lepton vs leading-particle selection not separated (lepton leads in 71 %). ParticleNet's flag effect does not pass through the kNN graph. One file, one seed. Conclusion: in the ParT and ParticleNet models that receive particle-ID inputs, removing the electron or muon flag from one particle moves about 99 % of H→ℓνqq′ jets to other classes; in each ParT model one head of the final class-token block reads that particle and has the largest single-head effect. The lepton also reaches the output through the particle blocks, and the two routes back each other up: blocking either alone changes at most 11 % of calls. The three-head circuit calls almost all leptonic-top jets H→ℓνqq′ and is no better than random head sets on class-balanced accuracy; a fresh-sample rerun is pending | new C15 (0.5); node thesis: "e1 ≫ e2" supported; "≤ 3 class heads" partly (one dominant head, redundant particle-block route); "literal circuit keeps lvqq accuracy" recall only, specificity not; "ParT_kin unaffected by e1, affected by e3/e4" supported; alternative "spread over many heads" partly supported |

Heads full: 0 0.49 [0.42, 0.56], 2 0.61 [0.53, 0.68] (p_Holm 0.008); top 3 (2, 0, 4) on held-out half 1.22 vs random 0.51 (p 0.018); kin heads 4, 3, 2. **P33v rerun** (WToQQ_117 + ZToQQ_117, 300 random rows each, default_rng(7117), N 600, 6,000 moved particles, float64 pair features; 10-class 150/class rng 7118): accuracy W/Z full 77.5 %, kin 63.5 %; 10-class 86.6 / 74.1 % (74.8 % float64). Share of even mean square from ΔR_nn < 0.01: full 0.967 [0.937, 0.981], kin 0.857 [0.775, 0.913]. Smoothness all particles full 0.119 [0.080, 0.181] (contains 0.09), kin 0.240 [0.168, 0.365]; linearised 0.079 / 0.184; input 0.64; below 0.003 full 0.034 vs input 0.49; 0.01–0.03 bin kin net − lin 0.059 [0.015, 0.116], p 0.001. Linearised/net even mean square 0.36 [0.15, 0.65] (full), 0.40 [0.26, 0.59] (kin), R² 0.07 / 0.09 (odd R² 0.95 / 0.64 at 0.003). Cluster/single radius 0.05: full 0.162 [0.053, 0.367], kin 0.147, input 0.077; radius 0.01: full 0.648 [0.487, 0.807], kin 0.818 [0.553, 1.198], input 0.394. Heads full 0 = 0.475, 2 = 0.554 (p_Holm 0.008); heads 0 + 2 edited 0.767 [0.686, 0.833] of even, 0.63 of odd; both reset leaves 0.075. Heads kin float64: 3 = 0.477, 2 = 0.360, 5 = 0.271, 4 = 0.000. Float32 vs float64 pair features: kin 17/1,500 10-class calls changed (11 vs 1 correctness changes, McNemar p 0.006), full 0/1,500 | corrected reading (P33v): the even pair-route response to a 0.01 rad single-particle move sits in particles with a neighbour closer than 0.01 rad (97 % full, 86 % kin of the even mean square); moving the particle with every neighbour within 0.05 rad leaves 15–16 %, with neighbours within 0.01 rad 65–82 %, so pairs out to about 0.05 rad carry it. The first-order (linearised) network on the exact pair-feature changes gives 36–40 % of the even mean square (R² < 0.1): ln ΔR's log nonlinearity sets which particles respond, but most of the even response is the network's own nonlinear response to large pair-feature changes of close pairs. P33's "non-smoothness inherited from ln ΔR alone" and "no network nonlinearity needed" not supported (the untrained network is not a no-nonlinearity control; bin pattern does not follow the input on fresh rows). No single channel carries ≥ 70 % (full kT 0.44; channel-only edits are non-additive, so shares are not a decomposition). ParT_full pair-bias heads 0 and 2 carry 77 % of the even and 63 % of the odd pair response; ParT_kin without rounding uses heads 3, 2, 5 (P33's head 4 was the rounding term). Float32 pair-feature rounding changes 1.1 % of ParT_kin's 10-class calls and none of ParT_full's. Conclusion: when one particle in a W or Z jet is moved by 0.01 rad, ParT's response through its pair features comes mostly from particles that have a neighbour closer than about 0.01 rad, and in ParT_full it passes through two of the eight pair-bias heads; both reproduce on a fresh sample. A linearised copy of the network, given the same input changes, produces only about a third of the even response, so the network's own nonlinearity makes most of it, not the logarithm of the pair distance alone. Carrying only the neighbours closer than 0.01 rad removes 20–35 % of the response; ParT_kin's float32 rounding changes about 1 % of its 10-class calls | new C16 (0.45); refines C14 (the non-smooth pair term is a close-pair effect read by 2–3 pair-bias heads, mostly network nonlinearity) and C1 (the pair-bias formula's response to moves is concentrated in heads 0 and 2 of ParT_full); node thesis: "≤ 3 heads" and "neighbour-holding removes most" supported (radius 0.05; geometric at radius 0.05, not at 0.01); "one channel ≥ 70 %" excluded; "hinge-specific non-smoothness" not separated; P33's "ln ΔR alone" reading refuted by P33v |

Intact network (all 80 heads kept), recall and t→bℓν→H→ℓνqq′: all MLPs 0.980 / 0.010; class-block MLPs at mean 0.975 [0.964, 0.984] / 0.006; particle-block MLPs at mean 0.934 / 0.136; all MLPs at mean 0.859. b2h1 literal circuit: AUC 0.922 vs rest, 0.426 vs t→bℓν; + class MLPs recall 0.812, t→bℓν 0.894; fixed threshold (10 % of reference jets called) recall 0.368 [0.339, 0.400], t→bℓν 0.496, no-lepton jets 0.000. Among 16 class heads only b2h1 gives recall > 0.01 with class MLPs kept (b2h6, b2h8 0.00 at AUC 0.91); random 3-head sets without b2h1 ≤ 0.009 (top-3 recall p 0.016); top-3 AUC p 0.082 (P34 0.010); ParT_kinpid only b2h4 (0.62) | corrected reading (P34v): on two fresh samples, removing the e/μ flag from the lepton changes 97.8–98.7 % of ParT_full's H→ℓνqq′ calls against 0.7–1.3 % for a matched-pT hadron flag change (0.34 % on a tight pT match), and the e↔μ swap 0.07–0.2 %, so the call depends on flag presence, not flavour; a muon flag on a hadron in a no-lepton jet raises H→ℓνqq′ calls from 0.16 to 18.3 %. The lepton reaches the class token through redundant head routes (class heads 13 %, particle heads 7 %, both 96 %). A literal head circuit (only b2h1, all else at mean) keeps a lepton-presence score (AUC 0.92) that the flag removal lowers and that does not separate t→bℓν (AUC 0.43); its argmax call is constant (recall 0) because of the offset of the mean-ablated logits, and a fixed threshold gives recall 0.37. In that reduced network the two class-block MLPs turn b2h1's output (not that of other class heads with equal AUC) into the call. In the intact network the class-block MLPs are not needed (recall 0.980 → 0.975 at their mean); the particle-block MLPs are needed to keep t→bℓν out (0.010 → 0.136). The top-k AUC advantage over random sets is not robust (p 0.010 → 0.082). P34's "the heads carry the lepton signal and the class-block MLPs compute the call" holds only for the reduced network. Conclusion: removing the lepton label from one particle changes about 98 % of ParT's H→ℓνqq′ decisions (under 1 % for a matched control), and adding a muon label to a hadron in a jet with no lepton creates the decision in 18 % of jets, so the label is necessary and partly sufficient. A circuit made only of the lepton-reading attention head still scores lepton presence but cannot tell these jets from leptonic top jets; in the intact network the units that make the decision are not found yet | C15 refined (v2, 0.55): flag dependence replicated on two fresh samples with a clean control and a sufficiency edit (supports); P34's head-circuit and class-MLP mechanism readings not supported for the intact network (weakens the circuit part); method: literal circuits report a threshold read-out and AUC next to argmax recall |

Offset c = pT-weighted mean (Δη, Δφ) about the stored axis; tails = top 1 / 2 / 5 % of \|c\| per class; matched low-\|c\| partners (same class, nearest in log m, log pT, N). Edits on stored (Δη, Δφ), pT and particle masses fixed: rec (shift by −c), ref (+c), rs (\|c\| in a random direction), rot (rotation about the axis by U(60°, 300°)), matched dilations k = √(M2_edit/M2). R(e) = mean read-out change / (κ · mean ½Δlog M2(e)), κ = pooled slope under matched dilations; R_nat = R(rec); read-out W-vs-Z log-odds on true W/Z jets (primary) and a ridge log m probe. Median \|c\| 0.0077 rad (all), 0.030 (tail5), 0.051 (tail1); predicted ½Δlog M2 under rec −0.0068 (tail5). Observational pairs +0.05 [−0.44, 0.61] vs edit prediction −0.04. **P36v** (_119, default_rng(3690), 8,000 W + 8,000 Z + 1,000 per other class, N 24,000; tail5 800 W/Z jets; P1 accuracy 85.93 / 74.46 / 84.57 / 70.35 %): momentum-axis offset from the stored axis (tail5 median) 0.0007 rad unedited, 0.026 after rec; R(rec) / R(rs): ParT_full tail5 1.05 [0.74, 1.40] / 0.35 [−0.06, 0.76] (p 0.007), all 0.94 [0.83, 1.06] / 0.82 (p 0.17); ParT_kin tail5 −0.08 [−0.74, 0.68], all 0.75 [0.21, 1.38] / −0.18 (P23 prediction −0.46); PN_full tail5 0.87 [0.12, 1.63], all 0.79 [0.65, 0.95]; PN_kin tail5 1.55 [0.84, 2.33] / 0.27 (p 0.017), all 0.67. Tail5 calls changed rec / rs / dil_rec / φ mirror / flip / rot: ParT_full 2.4 / 2.4 / 0.6 / 9.6 / 11.9 / 10.6 %; ParT_kin 9.3 / 7.9 / 3.9 / 2.9 / 20.5 / 17.4 %; PN_full 3.2 / 4.0 / 0.8 / 15.1 / 17.1 / 17.7 %; PN_kin 4.7 / 7.1 / 1.2 / 2.9 / 10.2 / 12.9 %; φ mirror lowers tail5 accuracy 79.4 → 75.3 % (ParT_full), 76.9 → 70.6 % (PN_full), kin models unchanged; rec vs mirror (kin) p_Holm 4e-12 (ParT_kin), 8e-7 (PN_kin). L-GATr, untrained probe and observational pairs not re-checked | corrected reading (P36v): recentring naturally off-centre W/Z jets at fixed invariant mass moves ParT_full's W/Z log-odds as a real mass change of equal ½Δlog M2 does (R(rec) 1.05 on 800 top-5 % jets, 0.94 on all W/Z jets), so ParT reads the moment about the stored axis, not about the pT centroid. The recentred jets are not natural: their momentum direction sits about 0.025 rad off the stored axis (0.0007 rad in unedited jets), so this is an artificial axis shift of natural size and direction; unedited jets were not tested. On the top 5 % R(rec) exceeds the random-shift R (0.35, p 0.007); on all W/Z jets they agree. ParticleNet 0.7–0.8 on all W/Z jets. Rotation about the axis is not a null (10–20 % of calls, mass not exactly kept); for the full-input models the φ mirror and the 180° flip also change 10–18 % of calls (some φ-orientation-dependent input, not identified); for the kin models the mirror is a near-null (2.9 %) and recentring exceeds it. ParT_kin's log-odds response does not follow the P23 no-odd-term prediction. L-GATr (interim checkpoint) 0.2–0.3, not re-checked. Conclusion: moving a jet's particles so that their pT-weighted centre lands on the stored axis, at fixed jet mass, changes ParT's W-vs-Z score about as much as a real mass change of the same size in the axis moment (ratio about 1.0). So ParT's mass sense is measured about the stored axis, also for offsets of the size real jets have; because these offsets are small, the effect corresponds to a mass shift near 1 % and changes a few percent of calls on the most off-centre jets. The edited jets are no longer natural (their momentum direction moves off the stored axis), so the effect on unedited jets is not measured | supports C3 (R ≈ 1 at natural offset sizes; on-manifold-invariance alternative not supported); new C18 (0.5); refines the control set (rotation, φ mirror and flip are not nulls in the full-input models); C6 interim only |

Unedited accuracy: P1 sample ParT_full 0.8593, ParT_kin 0.7447; this sample 0.871 [0.864, 0.877] / 0.754; top recall 0.958 / 0.905. Mean ablation = a head's output replaced by its mean over REF. d_QCD = Δ log(p_t/p_QCD) on true top jets. Single heads (full): b1h8 −1.51 [−1.56, −1.46], b1h4 −1.58, b1h3 −0.62, b1h1 −0.42, b2h1 −0.37 (Holm p 0.008); 57/80 significant lowering; largest single head 15 % of summed negative effects, 7 heads hold 50 %. Joint knock-out share (drop from k heads / drop from all 80, all-80 d_QCD −9.77): top-5 0.58 vs random 5 of 80 0.05; kin top-10 0.81 vs 0.11. Untrained: max single-head 0.28. 80 × 10 recall map: 39/800 BH cells (max \|Δ recall\| 0.113); permuted null 91. Top-5 sets by own-class log-odds: Jaccard top–W/Z 0.20, top–lepton 0.50 (random 95th pct 0.20); 48/50 slots class-block heads. Literal circuits (all other heads and all MLPs at mean): k = 5 balanced 0.180 vs random-of-80 p 0.010; with class-block MLPs kept k = 10 0.386, top recall 0.139. Edit: b-prong (third exclusive-kT subjet, other two nearest 80.4 GeV) merged into one particle at fixed jet four-momentum, 705 top jets: Δℓ_t −6.57 [−6.95, −6.21], top calls 0.948 → 0.460 (McNemar 354/10); pT-matched W-prong merge −3.15, whole W subjet −3.25, a − b −3.43 (p 5e-5); count-matched b2 −4.12; top-5 heads patched carry 0.66 / 0.57 / 0.53 of edits a / b / c (random 0.06). P37v (fresh _118, default_rng(3790), EVAL 300/class, 1,000 extra top jets, ranking frozen; jobs c-p37-verify, c-p37-verify2): ranking Spearman 0.99, same top-5; top-5 share 0.590 [0.585, 0.595]; all 16 class-token heads 1.000 of the drop and of every edit; class-matched random 5-sets (same number of class-token heads) 0.208 (p95 0.402), p 0.005; block-matched 0.330, p 0.01; kin top-10 0.808 vs class-matched 0.388; joint knock-out lowers ℓ_t by z −3.6 (full, Holm 0.060) and −3.2/−3.3 (kin, Holm 0.040) vs 500 class-matched sets, other classes \|z\| ≤ 2.1; literal (all MLPs ablated) vs class-matched p 0.12–0.22; class-MLP variant p 0.03–0.04 (balanced 0.34–0.38, top recall ≤ 0.12); edits a −6.10, b −3.17, c −3.17, a − b −2.93 [−3.45, −2.48]; patch shares 0.66 / 0.57 / 0.54 vs class-matched 0.19–0.21 (p 0.01); kin top-5 patch 0.37 vs 0.20 (p 0.18) | Corrected reading (P37v): five ranked class-token heads (b1h8, b1h4, b1h3, b1h1 of class block 1; b2h1, the head that attends to leptons) remove 59 % of the top-vs-QCD drop of the all-80 ablation, against 21 % for random sets of five class-token heads (the fair null, since all jet information passes through the 16 class-token heads) and 33 % for block-matched sets. Removing them lowers mainly the top score (z −3.2 to −3.6; Holm 0.04–0.06). Kept alone, they do not give the top call (top recall ≤ 0.12–0.14); with all MLPs ablated they do not beat class-matched random sets. Merging the b-prong lowers the top log-odds about twice as much as matched W-prong merges, and the same five heads carry 54–66 % of every prong-merge response. Conclusion: five attention heads in ParT's final class-token stage, where one summary token reads all particles, carry 59 % of the top-quark score drop, against 21 % for five random heads from that stage; they also carry two thirds of the response when the b-quark prong is merged into one particle. Their removal mainly affects the top class, but the per-class head sets are drawn from one shared pool of class-block heads and the specificity is only marginally significant after correction. Kept alone, these heads do not reproduce the top call, so they are not a stand-alone circuit | refines (C17 split: concentration supported 0.6; literal circuit not above class-matched sets; class specificity mixed 0.3) |

Read-out d = ℓ_Z − ℓ_W; calibration: per-jet slope under ±3 % angular dilation. P1-sample accuracy 85.93 / 74.47 / 84.56 / 70.36 %, L-GATr 84.27 % (N 3,000). (a) top vs bottom quintile of centroid offset \|c\| (c = pT-weighted centre of the particles in (η, φ) minus the stored axis), matched in m, pT, N, M2c (spread about the centroid): 34,414 pairs, ParT_full gap −0.31 [−0.35, −0.27] vs H1 (axis mass m_A) +0.029, R_nat −10.6 [−12.2, −9.1] (R_nat = (gap − invariant-mass prediction)/(m_A prediction − invariant-mass prediction)); other nets −8.4 to −12.0, L-GATr −18.2; \|η_jet\| imbalance 4.45 SD; with \|η\| matched 718 pairs R_nat +4.5 to +6.2; H0 and H1 each rejected (p ≤ 1e-4). (b) top 20 % off-centre jets (N 4,000), ParT_full: recentring (shift by −c) R 1.04 [0.92, 1.17]; iso shift (length \|c\| at 120° to c, M2 exactly fixed, momentum direction moved as far as recentring) iso/rec 0.19 [0.07, 0.32] (p 2e-4); all jets 0.06; PN_full 0.16, PN_kin 0.08; L-GATr rec R ≈ 0.17 (dilation 1.04); rebuilding the stored axis after recentring restores \|c\| 0.0130 rad (degenerate edit). (c) φ mirror calls changed, mirror → mirror + charge + d0 flipped: ParT_full 7.82 → 2.80 % (McNemar p_Holm 1e-133), PN_full 11.83 → 3.36 %; kin 1.6–1.7 %; dz flip p_Holm 1.0. (d) P31 final, R φ+ / φ− / η+: m10 (10 classes, 5.0M jets) 0.35 / 0.41 / 0.42; L (3 classes, 50 epochs) 0.29 / 0.31 / 0.35; released 0.72–0.87; per-particle route share m10 0.36, L 0.27 (released 0.83); all vs arm t p_Holm 0.0075 (15 tests). P39v (fresh _116, default_rng(3995), 30k W + 30k Z, mirror on 800 × 10; job c-p39-verify): (a) 8,932 pairs, \|η\| SMD 4.65, R_nat −10.9 [−14.0, −8.1]; moving the off-centre jet to its partner's η_jet (particle Δη, Δφ, pT kept) removes 87–172 % of the gap (p_Holm 0.0016); η law c_η = −tanh\|η_jet\| M2/(2 M0) R² 0.954. (b) N 12,000: rec R 0.94 [0.86, 1.01]; iso/rec 0.06 [−0.04, 0.14] (p iso = 0: 0.22); perp shift (90°, raises M2) perp/rec −0.75 [−0.88, −0.64]; M2-raising shifts R 0.71–0.84; joint fit mass coefficient 0.82 [0.78, 0.85], displacement coefficient 0.005 [−0.016, 0.028]; PN_full iso/rec 0.10, perp/rec −0.85. (c) ParT_full 7.74 → 2.78 % (p_Holm 4e-55), PN_full 11.62 → 3.15 % (1.5e-117), kin 1.58 / 1.45 % | Corrected reading (P39v): unedited off-centre and centred jets differ in \|η_jet\| because a real jet's pT centroid sits off its momentum direction by c_η ≈ −tanh\|η_jet\| M2/(2 M0) (pseudorapidity is not linear in p_z), so the natural comparison cannot identify the axis-moment effect. Edits: keeping M2 fixed while moving the momentum direction gives 0.06–0.19 of the recentring response (consistent with 0); a shift of equal length that raises M2 gives −0.75 of it; momentum-axis displacement is excluded as the source of P36's R ≈ 1. Rigid shifts cannot separate M2 from the centroid offset \|c\| (M2 = M2c + M0\|c\|²); M2 increases are followed at 0.71–0.84 of a real mass change. The full models' φ-mirror dependence comes from the joint sign of Δφ, charge and d0 (a physical mirror flips all three). P31: no arm reaches R 0.5. Conclusion: comparing unedited off-centre jets with centred jets of equal mass cannot test ParT's axis dependence, because the off-centre jets sit at larger \|η\| and every network, including the axis-blind L-GATr, scores them differently for that reason. Edits show the response follows the particles' spread about the stored axis rather than the momentum direction leaving the axis (0.06–0.19 of the response when only the direction moves). Rigid shifts cannot tell the spread apart from the centroid offset, and on real jets the offset is set by the jet's \|η\| and width, so its effect on real calls is inferred, not observed | refines (C18 v2, 0.65; new C20, 0.7; C10 v5, 0.65) |
| P39v | C18, C3, D4 | skeptic: fresh sample 3× larger; perp-shift control; joint fit of mass and displacement terms; η transport of the off-centre jet; η law of the centroid offset | done (reproduced with corrections; confidence 0.75) | see P39 row; interp/p39v_verify.py; job c-p39-verify (V100, 16 min); no code bug (iso keeps M2 to 1e-11, unedited momentum-axis offset 0.0002 rad); ParT_kin unresolved under these small rigid edits; L-GATr and P31 not re-run (JSON spot-checked); P39 mirror accuracies raw, not class-balanced; PN_full charge flip alone not distinguishable from mirror alone on the fresh sample (p_Holm 0.65) | iso/rec 0.19 → 0.06 [−0.04, 0.14] on the larger fresh sample; perp/rec −0.75 excludes momentum-axis displacement and a linear-in-displacement response (p_Holm 0.003); the \|η\| confound gets a causal test (η transport) and an explanation (η law). Conclusion: the main P39 results reproduce; the offset of real jets is a pseudorapidity coordinate effect tied to \|η\| and width, and rigid edits favour the spread reading over the momentum-direction reading without separating spread from centroid offset | refines (C18) |
| P40 | new C19 (loop C round C9, exploratory) | are the 2 × 512 hidden neurons of ParT's class-token MLPs individually readable and causally meaningful, more than directions of a random rotation of the same hidden space | done (round C9); verified with corrections (P40v, confidence 0.5) | jobs anrunw-ai-c-p40-run (GPU, 702 s), c-p40-plot; interp/p40_neuron_atlas.py, p40_plot.py. Sample: test_20M <class>_110 (no _126), 2,000 random rows per class (default_rng(4040)), 10,000 labelling + 10,000 held out. Unedited accuracy: P1 sample 85.93 / 74.47 %; held-out 86.11 / 73.80 %; class-block re-run max \|ΔL\| 0. Each unit labelled with the best of 22 labels (Spearman ρ with 12 observables: N, log pT, log m, log m_A, τ21, τ32, D2, leading fraction, charged / neutral counts, leptons, max \|d0\|; 2(AUC − 0.5) for 10 classes), scored on held-out jets, BH over 512 × 22; clean = above the untrained net's 95th pct (0.276 full, 0.359 kin) and BH q < 0.05; selective = clean and label family leads the next by ≥ 0.1. Clean fraction (post-GELU): full neurons 0.868, rotations 0.985–1.000, untrained 0.051, permuted 0.024; kin 0.574 vs 0.728–0.983. Selective: full 0.488 [0.458, 0.519] vs rotations 0.492–0.501 (p 0.73); kin 0.312 vs 0.347–0.512; untrained/permuted ≤ 0.008. Steering +2 s.d., 300 random clean units: direction-correct full 0.533 [0.477, 0.589] vs rotated 0.470, matched-variance other-label 0.523; kin 0.537 vs 0.523; median \|Δℓ\| 0.001–0.003. Post-hoc subset \|Δℓ\| ≥ 0.05: full 18/24, kin 31/36 vs rotated 24/54, 21/45. Calibration (all angles × 1.05, ΔℓW −0.144 full, −0.170 kin): R = patched change / real change; b1n92 0.117, b1n108 0.089; 18 mass units jointly R_W 0.303 [0.278, 0.335] (full), 0.173 (kin) vs random −0.03. All 1,024 units at mean: 86.11 → 82.31 % (W recall 0.793 → 0.445). Per-class unit circuits (attention kept, not literal): ≤ 1.5 points, p ≥ 0.048 (20 random sets). P40v (fresh _111, default_rng(4141), 10k + 10k; job c-p40-verify): held-out 86.61 / 75.18 %; selective full 0.485 vs rotations 0.46–0.53, kin 0.304 vs 0.31–0.54; selective among clean full 0.563 vs ≤ 0.533, kin 0.574 vs ≤ 0.547 (p 0.09 per comparison); steering full 0.583 [0.527, 0.638] vs rotated 0.380 (own step) / 0.383 (equal step), Fisher p 1.3e-6; kin 0.513 vs 0.513 / 0.517; pre-registered subset 18/24, 31/36 vs equal-step rotated 11/24, 19/36; lepton units b2n153 Δℓ +0.030, b2n249 +0.299, b1n138 +0.0007 (ablation 0–1 calls); 18 mass units R_W 0.284 [0.263, 0.308] / 0.175 vs 20 random neuron sets max 0.16 / 0.06 (p 0.048); b1n92 + b1n108 0.188; freshly mass-labelled set 0.084 / 0.163; oracle (18 most-shifted units) −0.73 / −0.44 (R is a net, not a share); rigid φ + 0.06: W → Z 11.5 → 31.9 % (full), 16.5 → 36.1 % (kin), mass-unit R_W 0.261 / 0.211 vs random 0.015 / 0.005 | Corrected reading (P40v): the clean criterion saturates; the selective fraction of neurons is within the rotation range (full) or below it (kin, a threshold effect: among clean units neurons are more selective than all 10 rotation seeds). Steering one neuron moves its class the predicted way in 53–58 % (full) and 51–54 % (kin), median \|Δℓ\| ≈ 0.001; rotated directions 38–47 % (full), 51–52 % (kin). A pre-registered high-impact subset replicates (75–86 % vs 46–53 %). P40's 18 axis-mass units reproduce 28 % (full) and 17 % (kin) of the net W log-odds change under a real mass change, and 26 % / 21 % under a rigid φ shift at fixed invariant mass; 0.19 of the 0.28 sits in b1n92 + b1n108, which the fresh labelling calls W-class units. C19 as stated (≥ 2× rotations, > 70 % correct steering) excluded on both samples. Conclusion: as a set, the neurons in ParT's final class-token layers are about as readable as random rotated directions of the same space, and nudging one neuron moves its labelled class the predicted way only slightly more often than a random direction does, usually by a negligible amount. A small minority have reproducible causal roles; a few that follow the jet mass measured about the jet axis reproduce about a quarter of the W-versus-other response to a real mass change or to a sideways φ shift. Which units count as mass units changes between samples, and much of that effect sits in two units | weakens (C19 0.1; refined C19r 0.45) |

| P43 | C18, C3 (loop C round C10, pre-registered) | does ParT read the spread about the stored axis M2 as one number, or the spread about the pT centroid (M2c) and the centroid offset c separately (M2 = M2c + M0\|c\|²); compensated two-particle edits in ParT_full/kin and ParticleNet_full/kin | done (round C10); offset reading refuted by P43v (confidence 0.2; spread part reproduces) | job c-p43-run (GPU, exit 0); interp/p43_spread_vs_centroid.py. Sample: val_5M WToQQ/ZToQQ _120 (test_20M ends at _119), 10,000 random rows each, default_rng(4343); 4,592 / 20,000 jets admit a similar-pT pair (pT ratio ≥ 0.6, top 8). Definitions: M0 = Σ pT; c = pT centroid of (Δη, Δφ) about the stored axis; M2 = Σ pT r² about the axis; M2c = M2 − M0\|c\|². Edits: E1 two particles moved oppositely along their joining line by ±t/pT (c fixed, M2 = M2c changes by ±2/5/10 %); E2 the pair translated so c moves δ = 0.01/0.02/0.04 rad, M2c restored; E3 as E2 with the stored axis moved along (c and M2 unchanged); E2 − E3 = offset term; E4 matched angular dilation (same Δlog M2); E5 same pair and displacement size with c and M2 fixed (null). Read-out d = ℓ_Z − ℓ_W; R = Σ Δd(edit) / Σ Δd(E4). P1-sample accuracy 85.93 / 74.47 / 84.56 / 70.36 % (ParT_full / kin / PN_full / kin); kept jets 80.5 / 70.8 / 78.2 / 68.5 %; untrained 10.1 %, permuted 9.7 %. ParT_full: E1 raise 0.95 [0.90, 1.00], lower 0.92 [0.87, 0.97], E2 − E3 0.31 [0.28, 0.34], E5 0.03 [−0.01, 0.07]; PN_full 0.91 / 0.99 / 0.30 / −0.05; ParT_kin 1.11 / 0.54 / 0.37 / 0.37; PN_kin 0.81 / 0.58 / −0.18 / 0.12; R(E1 raise) − R(offset) +0.61 to +0.99, Holm p 0.002 (4 models); W → Z ParT_full true W 11.1 % → 25.8 % (E1 +10 %) vs 24.7 % (E4). P43v (val_5M W/Z _121, rng 4344, 4,502 kept + 15,498 dropped jets; job c-p43-verify): ParT_full E1 raise 0.97 [0.92, 1.02], lower 0.86 [0.80, 0.91], E2 − E3 0.34, E5 0.10 [0.06, 0.14]; new E6 = rigid offset shift of all axis-relative coordinates on unedited jets with the stored axis moved so lab four-vectors, invariant mass and pair features are fixed (Δlog M2c = 0, \|Δlog m\| ≤ 8e-4): E6e (E2 direction) 0.91 [0.88, 0.94], E6r (random directions) 0.73 [0.71, 0.76], per dose 0.005–0.06 rad 0.72–0.76, dropped jets 0.80 [0.78, 0.81]; (E2 − E3) − E6e −0.58 [−0.61, −0.54] (Holm p 0.002); E1 raise − E6e +0.06 [−0.00, 0.12] (Holm p 0.057). PN_full E6e 0.20, E6r 0.61, 0.73 → 0.47 over 0.005–0.06 rad; ParT_kin E6r 0.74, PN_kin 0.61. ParT_full W → Z under E6r 0.06 rad 9.5 → 28.7 % (E4 36.8 %) | corrected reading (P43v): spread changes about a fixed centroid give ParT_full and PN_full 0.92–0.97 of a matched dilation; P43's offset weight 0.31 was measured on jets whose two hardest particles had already been moved by 0.03–0.13 rad (E3), a deformation that alone changes 46 % of ParT_full calls; on unedited jets the offset counts 0.73–0.91 for ParT_full, flat in dose; PN_full 0.20–0.61, direction- and dose-dependent. Conclusion: ParT_full responds to moving two hard particles apart at a fixed jet centre almost exactly as to a real mass change of the same size, and to moving the jet centre off the stored axis at about three quarters of that; ParticleNet_full gives the off-axis shift less and variable weight; jets without two similar-pT hard particles were tested only for the rigid shift | refines (C18 → C18 v3) |

Each token: 16 multivector channels (grades 0 scalar, 1 vector, 2 bivector, 3 axial vector, 4 pseudoscalar) + 32 auxiliary scalars s. Mean ablation at each block output: g0 alone 81.6–84.1 %; s 40–70 %; inv (g0 + s) 24–47 % (extra drop vs 48 random components +35.5 [33.2, 37.7] at b5, +56.1 at b11, Holm p < 1e-10); g1/g3 14–33 % at blocks 0–2, ≥ 79 % from block 6; g2, g4 ≤ 2 points. Longitudinal boost (rapidity y; all m_ij fixed), calls changed at y 0.1/0.3/0.5: physical A 0.65/1.70/2.58 %; time-spurion route D 1.74/4.78/7.89 %; scalar-feature route F 1.44/4.62/8.39 %; spurion and particles boosted with features held C 0.13/0.17/0.11 % (floor 0.02 %, φ rotation 0.07–0.09 %). log m ridge probe R² 0.98 → 0.92, dilation gain 0.92–1.03, φ + 0.06 0.000; untrained R² 0.88 → 0.78. P44v (val_5M _120, 500 rows / class, default_rng(7777), N 5,000; jobs c-p44-verify, c-p44-verify-an): unedited 85.20 % [84.22, 86.20]; ablations reproduce (s b0 53.5, inv b3 25.0, g1 b0 13.9 %); redundancy: g0 removed after s costs 6.1 [5.5, 6.8] (b0), 24.9 (b5), 43.3 [42.3, 44.3] (b11) points, interaction > 0 at all 12 blocks (McNemar p < 1e-76); type-matched control (16 of 32 s): g0 − control +4.8 [4.1, 5.5] at b3 (mean), −3.7 [−4.8, −2.5] at b11 (resample); boost y +0.5 A 2.84 % [2.38, 3.32], C 0.14 %, D 7.62 %, F 8.80 %, F_logE 8.92 %, F_rest 0.16 %; y −0.5 A 2.76 %; cos(ΔD, ΔF) −0.60 [−0.61, −0.59], ‖ΔD + ΔF‖/‖ΔA‖ 2.2; probe R² trained inv 0.975 → 0.906 vs untrained 0.864 → 0.763; s 0.97 → 0.88, g0 0.95 → 0.74 | corrected reading (P44v): the decision uses the invariant channels, grade 0 and the auxiliary scalars holding overlapping copies (grade 0 is a backup carrier, not unused); g1/g3 drops at blocks 0–2 equal random equal-size sets; the beam boost acts only through the time reference and the log-E inputs, anti-aligned (cos −0.6) but not additive; joint boost at the floor is an implementation check; the trained net reads log m better than an untrained copy; C6 untested (no M2-changing iso-mass edit). Conclusion: in our Lorentz-equivariant tagger the class decision runs through its Lorentz-invariant channels, with two overlapping copies; a boost along the beam changes about 3 % of calls, only through the inputs that fix the lab frame; whether it responds to axis-moment changes at fixed invariant mass is the open P6 test | refines (C6 scope; new C22) |

Affine map t_i = W x_i + b from PN EdgeConv-1/-2/-3 outputs (64/128/256 per particle) into the input of ParT particle block k = 1, 3, 5, 8 (12 points; pair bias from the real jet, mask kept) and PN pooled vector → ParT class-token vector (1 point); least-squares start, one pass of KL(p_ParT ‖ p_stitched) on 300,000 train_100M jets (2 random files / class, 15,000 random rows, default_rng(4545)). Controls: untrained PN, raw 17-input floor, ParT identity ceiling, 2nd seed. Evaluation P1 sample and val_5M _121 (1,000 / class, default_rng(4546)). R: ec1/ec2 → b1–b3 0.75–0.85; ec2/ec3 → b8 0.48–0.55; pooled 0.30–0.35; untrained 0.62–1.03. P45v (fresh 300k train jets default_rng(4747); val_5M _122, 2,000 / class, rng 4748, N 20,000; job anrunw-ai-c-p45-verify, 4th launch after GPU faults): ParT 86.42 %, R 0.791 [0.776, 0.807]; PN 84.50 %, R 0.505 [0.484, 0.524]; pn1 > b1 gap +1.08 [+0.80, +1.38] R 0.774; pn2 > b1 +1.34 R 0.814; pn3 > b1 +8.20 R 0.761; pn1 > b8 +9.22 R 0.773; pn2 > b8 +4.70 R 0.511; pn3 > b8 +5.96 R 0.492; u2 > b1 +9.24 R 0.677; u2 > b8 +39.65 R 0.949; raw > b1 +13.86 R 0.476; pooled +3.56 R 0.297; reverse stitches (ParT → PN, gap vs PN): pt1 > ec2 +2.96 R 0.751; pt8 > ec2 −0.05 R 0.770; pt1 > ec3 +2.49 R 0.832; pt8 > ec3 −0.61 R 0.702; ptcls > fc −1.53 R 0.796 (all vs PN Holm p 0.008); paired ΔR pn2 > b8 − u2 > b8 −0.438, pn2 > b1 − u2 > b1 +0.137, raw > b1 − pn2 > b1 −0.338 (Holm p 0.004); second training pass changes R by ≤ 0.03 | corrected reading (P45v): EC1/EC2 features replace ParT's tokens at blocks 1–3 at 1–2 points (untrained 9 points); EC3 loses 5–9. R is not set by the recipient alone: at block 1 the donor changes it (trained EC2 0.81, untrained 0.68, raw 0.48); ParT features fed through PN's EC2/EC3/pooling/head keep a ParT-sized R (0.70–0.83 vs PN 0.51); a PN-sized R appears only for trained EC2/EC3 read out through ParT block 8 + class blocks. PN's smaller response is associated with its first two EdgeConv layers plus a short read-out, not with EC3, pooling or the head. Conclusion: ParticleNet's per-particle features after its first or second graph layer can stand in for ParT's own at ParT's first attention blocks with one linear map at about 1 point of accuracy; the size of the response to a mass-preserving axis shift depends on both the features and the layers that read them, and ParticleNet's later layers do not by themselves make its response smaller; kin models and other read-outs untested | refines (A8; C5 note; new C21) |
